##### <span style="font-weight:normal">Figure 12. Quality loop </span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:
yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image246.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_2958"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">  
</span></span>Examples of quality management activities include:

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Assessing risks and implementing risk treatments to
ensure fit-for-purpose quality;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Setting quality criteria to be used in the process;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Setting process quality targets and monitoring
compliance;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Seeking and analysing user feedback;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Reviewing operations and documenting lessons
learned;
<span style="font-family:&quot;Segoe UI Symbol&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:
&quot;Segoe UI Symbol&quot;;mso-bidi-font-family:&quot;Segoe UI Symbol&quot;">•</span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial">
</span>Examining process metadata and quality indicators;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Internal or external auditing on the process.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image248.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_2985"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>Quality indicators support a process-oriented quality
management. A suggested list of quality indicators for phases and
sub-processes of the GSBPM as well as for the overarching quality and
metadata management processes can be found at the Quality Indicators for
the GSBPM – for Statistics derived from Surveys and Administrative Data
Sources<a href="#_ftn11" id="_ftnref11"
style="mso-footnote-id:ftn11"><sup><span
style="mso-special-character:footnote"><sup><span
style="font-size:12.0pt;line-height:103%;font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif;
mso-fareast-font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;;color:black;mso-font-kerning:0pt;
mso-ligatures:none;mso-ansi-language:EN-US;mso-fareast-language:EN-US;
mso-bidi-language:AR-SA">[11]</span></sup></span></sup></a>. Among
others, they can be used as a checklist to identify gaps and/or
duplication of work in the organisation.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

#### **<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
mso-fareast-font-family:Arial">Metadata Management </span>**

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image250.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_2998"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>Metadata has an important role and must be managed at an
operational level within the statistical production process. When
aspects of metadata management are considered at corporate or strategic
level (e.g. there are metadata systems that impact large parts of the
production system), it should be considered in the framework of the
GAMSO.<span style="font-size:10.5pt;mso-bidi-font-size:
12.0pt;line-height:103%;font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:
Arial;color:#333333"> </span><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image252.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3041"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>Good metadata management is essential for the efficient
operation of statistical business processes. Metadata are present in
every phase, either created, updated or carried forward from a previous
phase or reused from another business process. In the context of this
model, the emphasis of the overarching process of metadata management is
on the creation/revision, updating, use and archiving of statistical
metadata, though metadata on the different sub-processes themselves are
also of interest, including as an input for quality management. The key
challenge is to ensure that these metadata are captured as early as
possible, and stored and transferred from phase to phase alongside the
data they refer to. Metadata management strategy and systems are
therefore vital to the operation of this model, and these can be
facilitated by the GSIM.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image254.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3054"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>The GSIM is a reference framework of information objects,
which enables generic descriptions of the definition, management and use
of data and metadata throughout the statistical production process. The
GSIM supports a consistent approach to metadata, facilitating the
primary role for metadata, that is, that metadata should uniquely and
formally define the content and links between information objects and
processes in the statistical information system.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image256.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3063"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>The METIS Common Metadata Framework identifies the
following sixteen core principles for metadata management, all of which
are intended to be covered in the overarching metadata management
process, and taken into the consideration when designing and
implementing a statistical metadata system. The principles are presented
in four groups:

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<table class="TableGrid" data-border="0" data-cellspacing="0"
data-cellpadding="0" width="649"
style="width:486.85pt;margin-left:-5.75pt;border-collapse:collapse;mso-yfti-tbllook:
 1184;mso-padding-alt:1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 33%" />
<col style="width: 33%" />
<col style="width: 33%" />
</colgroup>
<tbody>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:0;mso-yfti-firstrow:yes;height:35.0pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>Metadata
handling</p>
<p><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span></p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; border-top: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>i.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 357.25pt; border-top: solid black 1.0pt; border-left: none; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>Statistical
Business Process Model: Manage metadata with a focus on the overall
statistical business process model;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:1;height:41.4pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 41.4pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 41.4pt"><p>ii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 41.4pt"><p>Active
not passive: Make metadata active to the greatest extent possible.
Active metadata are metadata that drive other processes and actions.</p>
<p>Treating metadata this way will ensure they are accurate and
up-to-date;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:2;height:27.6pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>iii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>Reuse:
Reuse metadata where possible for statistical integration as well as
efficiency reasons;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:3;height:32.3pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-top: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.3pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.3pt"><p>iv.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border-top: none; border-left: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.3pt"><p>Versions:
Preserve history (old versions) of metadata.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:4;height:48.8pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 48.8pt"><p>Metadata</p>
<p>Authority</p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 48.8pt"><p>i.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 48.8pt"><p>Registration:
Ensure the registration process (workflow) associated with each metadata
element is well documented so there is clear identification of
ownership, approval status, date of operation, etc.;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:5;height:27.6pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>ii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>Single
source: Ensure that a single, authoritative source (“registration
authority”) for each metadata element exists;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:6;height:27.6pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>iii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 27.6pt"><p>One
entry/update: Minimise errors by entering once and updating in one
place;<span style="mso-spacerun:yes">  </span></p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:7;height:46.25pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-top: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 46.25pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 46.25pt"><p>iv.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border-top: none; border-left: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 46.25pt"><p>Standards
variations: Ensure that variations from standards are tightly
managed/approved, documented and visible.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:8;height:76.4pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 76.4pt"><p>Relationship
to</p>
<p>Statistical Cycle /</p>
<p>Processes</p>
<p><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span></p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 76.4pt"><p>i.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span>ii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 76.4pt"><p>Integrity:
Make metadata-related work an integral part of business processes across
the organisation;<span style="mso-spacerun:yes">  </span></p>
<p>Matching metadata: Ensure that metadata presented to the end-users
match the metadata that drove the business process or were created
during the process;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:9;height:32.2pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-top: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.2pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.2pt"><p>iii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border-top: none; border-left: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 32.2pt"><p>Describe
flow: Describe metadata flow with the statistical and business processes
(alongside the data flow and business logic);</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:10;height:35.0pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>iv.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>Capture
at source: Capture metadata at their source, preferably automatically as
a by-product of other processes;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:11;height:87.65pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-top: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 87.65pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 87.65pt"><p>v.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border-top: none; border-left: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 87.65pt"><p>Exchange
and use: Exchange metadata and use them for informing both computer
based processes and human interpretation. The infrastructure for
exchange of data and associated metadata should be based on loosely
coupled components, with a choice of standard exchange languages, such
as XML.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:12;height:35.0pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>Users</p>
<p><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span></p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>i.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="bottom"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-top-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 35.0pt"><p>Identify
users: Ensure that users are clearly identified for all metadata
processes, and that all metadata capturing will create value for
them;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:13;height:69.0pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border-top: none; border-left: solid black 1.0pt; border-bottom: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 69.0pt"><p> </p></td>
<td width="32" data-valign="top"
style="width: 23.75pt; border: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 69.0pt"><p>ii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span></p></td>
<td width="476" data-valign="top"
style="width: 357.25pt; border: none; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 69.0pt"><p>Different
formats: The diversity of metadata is recognised and there are different
views corresponding to the different uses of the data. Different users
require different levels of detail. Metadata appear in different formats
depending on the processes and goals for which they are produced and
used;</p></td>
</tr>
<tr style="mso-yfti-irow:14;mso-yfti-lastrow:yes;height:59.9pt">
<td width="141" data-valign="top"
style="width: 105.85pt; border: solid black 1.0pt; border-top: none; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 59.9pt"><p> </p></td>
<td colspan="2" width="508" data-valign="top"
style="width: 381.0pt; border-top: none; border-left: none; border-bottom: solid black 1.0pt; border-right: solid black 1.0pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-left-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-bottom-alt: solid black .5pt; mso-border-right-alt: solid black .5pt; padding: 1.35pt 1.7pt 1.45pt 0in; height: 59.9pt"><p>iii.<span
style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
  mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"> </span>Availability: Ensure that
metadata are readily available and useable in the context of the users’
information needs (whether an internal or external user).</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**<span style="font-size:11.0pt;mso-bidi-font-size:
12.0pt;line-height:107%"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span></span>**

#### **<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;
mso-fareast-font-family:Arial">Data Management </span>**

**<span style="font-size:11.0pt;mso-bidi-font-size:12.0pt;line-height:
107%"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span></span>**

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image258.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3240"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>Data management is essential as data are produced within
many of the activities in the statistical business process and are the
key outputs. The main goal of data management is to ensure that data are
appropriately used and usable throughout their lifecycle. Managing data
throughout their lifecycle covers activities such as planning and
evaluation of data management processes as well as establishing and
implementing processes related to collection, organisation, use,
protection, preservation and disposal of the
data.<span style="mso-spacerun:yes">  </span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image260.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3250"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>How data are managed will be closely linked to the use of
the data, which in turn is linked to the statistical business process
where the data are created. Both data and the processes in which they
are created must be well defined in order to ensure proper data
management.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:
yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image262.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3257"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">  
</span></span>Examples of data management activities include:

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Establishing a governance structure and assigning
data stewardship responsibilities;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Designing data structures and associated data sets,
and the flow of data through the statistical business process;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Identifying database (repositories) to store the
data and administration of the database;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Documenting the data (e.g. registering and
inventorying data, classifying data according to content, retention or
other required classification);

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Determining retention periods of data;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Securing data against unauthorised access and use;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Safeguarding data against technological change,
physical media degradation, data corruption;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Performing data integrity checks (e.g. periodic
checks providing assurance about the accuracy and consistency of data
over its entire lifecycle);

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Performing disposition activities once the
retention period of the data is expired.

# <span id="_Toc53431">VII. <span style="mso-tab-count:1">    </span>Other Uses of the GSBPM</span><span style="mso-bookmark:_Toc53431"><span style="color:red"> </span></span>

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="mso-no-proof:yes"><img src="GSBPM_v5_1_files/image264.gif" v:shapes="Picture_x0020_3306"
width="26" height="12" /></span><span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span><span style="mso-tab-count:1">   
</span></span>The original aim of the GSBPM was to provide a basis for
statistical organisations to agree on standard terminology for
discussions on developing statistical metadata systems and processes.
However, as the model has developed, it has become increasingly used for
many other purposes, in particular, related to modernisation of official
statistics. Several papers describing actual and potential uses of the
GSBPM are available on the UNECE wiki
platform<a href="#_ftn12" id="_ftnref12"
style="mso-footnote-id:ftn12"><sup><span
style="mso-special-character:footnote"><sup><span
style="font-size:12.0pt;line-height:103%;font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif;
mso-fareast-font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;;color:black;mso-font-kerning:0pt;
mso-ligatures:none;mso-ansi-language:EN-US;mso-fareast-language:EN-US;
mso-bidi-language:AR-SA">[12]</span></sup></span></sup></a>. The list
below aims to highlight some current uses, and to inspire further ideas
on how the GSBPM can be used in practice.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing a structure for documentation of
statistical processes - The GSBPM can provide a structure for organising
and storing documentation within an organisation, promoting
standardisation and the identification of good practices;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Facilitating the sharing of statistical methods and
software - The GSBPM defines the components of statistical processes in
a way that not only encourages the sharing of methods and software tools
between statistical business processes, but also facilitates sharing
between different statistical organisations that apply the model;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Describing which standards are or could be used for
different phases of the statistical production process. For example,
Annex 2 of the SDMX 2.1 User Guide<sup>13</sup> explores how SDMX
applies to statistical work in the context of a business process model;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing a framework for process quality
assessment and improvement - If a benchmarking approach to process
quality assessment is to be successful, it is necessary to standardise
processes as much as possible. The GSBPM provides a mechanism to
facilitate this;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Better integrating work on statistical metadata and
quality - Linked to the previous point, the common framework provided by
the GSBPM can help to integrate international work on statistical
metadata with that on data quality by providing a common framework and
common terminology to describe the statistical business process;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing the underlying model for methodological
standards frameworks - Methodological standards can be linked to the
phase(s) or sub-process(es) they relate to and can then be classified
and stored in a structure based on the GSBPM;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Developing a business process model repository to
store process modelling outputs and allow them to be linked to the
statistical business process model;<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> 
</span>

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing an underlying model to design a training
framework and a set of standard terminology to describe skills and
expertise needed in the statistical production process;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Measuring operational costs - The GSBPM can be used
as a basis for measuring the costs of different parts of the statistical
business process. This helps to target modernisation activities to
improve the efficiency of the parts of the process that are most costly;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Measuring system performance - Related to the point
above on costs, the GSBPM can also be used to identify components that
are not performing efficiently, that are duplicating each other
unnecessarily, or that require replacing. Similarly, it can identify
gaps for which new components should be developed;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing a tool for aligning business processes of
providers of non-statistical data (e.g.

administrative data, geospatial data) facilitating communication between
statisticians and experts from other domains and for harmonising related
terminology;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing a tool to build capacity and to build
technical knowledge methodically by referring to each phase's details;

<span style="font-family:&quot;Arial&quot;,sans-serif;mso-fareast-font-family:Arial"><span style="mso-list:Ignore">•<span style="font:7.0pt &quot;Times New Roman&quot;">      
</span></span></span>Providing a tool for development/revision of
statistical classifications.

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>

# <span id="_Toc53432">Annex – List of Acronyms</span>

**API** – Application Programming Interface

**GAMSO** – Generic Activity Model for Statistical Organisations: A
reference framework describing and defining the activities that take
place within a typical statistical organisation

**GIS** – Geographic Information System

**GPS** – Global Positioning System

**GSBPM** – Generic Statistical Business Process Model: A flexible tool
to describe and define the set of business processes needed to produce
official statistics

**GSIM** – Generic Statistical Information Model: A reference framework
of information objects, which enables generic descriptions of the
definition, management and use of data and metadata throughout the
statistical production process

**HLG-MOS** – High-Level Group for the Modernisation of Official
Statistics<span style="mso-spacerun:yes">  </span>

**ICT** – Information and Communications Technology

**METIS** – The "brand name" for work on Statistical Metadata under the
Conference of European Statisticians

**OECD** – Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development

**SDMX** – Statistical Data and Metadata eXchange: A set of technical
standards and content-oriented guidelines, together with an IT
architecture and tools, to be used for the efficient exchange and
sharing of statistical data and metadata

**UNECE** – United Nations Economic Commission for Europe

**XML** – eXtensible Mark-up Language: A language that defines a set of
rules for encoding documents in a format that is both human-readable and
machine-readable

<span style="mso-spacerun:yes"> </span>
